### Relationship Tiers

| Tier | Score Range |
|------|------------|
| Nemesis | −60 to −100 |
| Rival | −30 to −59 |
| Acquaintance | 10 to 29 |
| Friend | 30 to 59 |
| Close Friend | 60 to 89 |
| Best Friend | 90 to 100 |

### Courtship & Marriage

Romance follows 6 stages: **Strangers → Interested → Flirting → Courting → Betrothed → Married**.

Key romantic actions:

| Action | Stage Required | Cost | Affection Gain |
|--------|---------------|------|----------------|
| Chat | Strangers | Free | 1–4 |
| Compliment | Strangers | Free | 2–6 |
| Gift | Interested | 0–100g | by gift |
| Serenade | Flirting | Free | 4–10 |
| Romantic Dinner | Courting | 50g | 8–15 |
| Propose | Courting (70+ affection) | 100g | 15–25 |

Gifts come from a list — Wildflowers (5g), Silver Necklace (50g), Gold Ring (100g), Written Poem (free, helped by intelligence), Fine Blade (80g) and Rare Book (40g). Pricier gifts win more affection, and each personality has favourites worth half again as much (bold and stoic types love a blade; witty ones a poem or book).

**Marriage benefits** (from `data/relationships.json`): +1 stamina, +10 social, +5 reputation with your spouse's kingdom, +3 renown, and kinship: that kingdom's lord thinks better of you (+20 opinion) for as long as the marriage lasts.

**Affairs.** Marriage doesn't stop you courting someone else. You can't propose, and spouse-only acts stay locked, but every successful romantic act carries a 30% chance that someone saw you. An *Affair* rumour then starts in your lover's town. It is talk, not a crime (no bounty, no hostile guards), and the townsfolk who hear it will tease you about it. If it reaches your spouse's town, or your spouse travels with you, your spouse will want a word (see below). A spouse may also sense that something is wrong even without the rumour.

### Matters of the Heart (and Friendship)

The people you know bring things to you. A notice tells you who and where (*"Mira has something to ask you, quietly. Find them in Tallow."*). They are tagged **💌 Wants a word** in Meet People and listed under **💌 Unfinished Business** in your Relationships panel. Settle a matter face to face: open their scene and their answers come first. Each person has at most one open matter, at most 3 are open at once, and each lasts **18 days**. A matter you ignore has its own cost.

| Matter | Who | Your answers | If ignored |
|---|---|---|---|
| 💰 **A Loan** (25–120g) | Friends (30+) | Lend: +8, and they repay within 20–40 days with 20% on top (more honest personalities are likelier to pay, and so are loyal ones: ±0.5% per point of loyalty from 50) · Refuse: −4 | −3 |
| 📉 **The Debt** | A friend who can't repay | Forgive: +12, +2 karma · Demand: half back and −10 on a charisma roll, −20 on a failure · Wait: 30 more days, +2 | Taken as forgiveness |
| 🤫 **A Confidence** | Close friends (60+) | They tell you whether a court scandal in their realm is **true or a lie** (see [Court Scandals & Whispers](#court-scandals--whispers)), or confide a sorrow: comfort them (+6) or deflect (−2) | Nothing |
| 🙏 **A Plea** (30–90g) | Friends (30+) | Pay: +12, +3 karma, more loyalty · Refuse: −6 | −8 |
| 🌹 **A Rival Suitor** | Someone you court (affection 25–79) | Gift (40g): +10 affection · Fight the suitor (strength roll): +15 affection and a *Duel Won* deed, or −5 affection and −15 health · Step aside: the romance ends, +5 | They marry the suitor: the romance is over, and they can no longer be courted |
| 👂 **What They Heard** | Friends (10+) whose town has heard a crime rumour about you | Deny: a lie, harder the worse the rumour is told there (a failed lie costs −18) · Admit: costs more with kind or gentle people, less with bold or ambitious ones, earns loyalty (+5), and they **trust your word** after: +15% to later denials and lies with them, until they catch you lying · Justify: the ambitious and bold respect it, the kind recoil. A slander from a rival is simply denied (+3), and they speak up for you: the slander is sung down in their town and loses 30% of its credit everywhere | −8 |
| 💢 **Jealousy** | Your spouse | Lie (worse odds if they heard the rumour) · End it: the romance they named ends (that lover −25); if they only sensed it, all your other romances end · Defy them: −20 affection. A failed lie costs −30. Below 15 affection, **they leave you** | −12 affection |
| 🗡️ **A Grudge** | Enemies (−30 or worse) | They spread a *Slander* rumour about you from their town. Make peace (30g): +30 · Threaten (strength roll): the slander stops | −10, and it goes cold: they will not be courted for 180 days |

**Loyalty.** Each person's loyalty to you (50 to start) rises when you bail out a kinsman (+10) or admit a hard truth (+5) and falls when they catch you lying (−5). It shifts the odds that they join your travel party (±0.6% per point from 50) and that a debt of theirs is repaid.

Good word travels too. When a deed of yours reaches the town of someone who likes you, they warm to you (+2) and tell everyone they know you.

### Children & Heirs

- Children can be born each year (25% probability), up to 6. Each is born with a random birth trait: Gifted (+1 all), Strong (+2 STR), Clever (+2 INT), Charming (+2 CHA), and others.
- Designate one as your **heir**. When you die you may still choose any child aged 16+.
- Open **Dynasty → 📖 Upbringing** on a child to raise them.

**Education focus (ages 6–16).** Choose one focus per child. Every 30 days the child grows toward it (a year is 120 days). Without a focus a child picks up a little of every skill (+0.12 a year) and no particular character.

| Focus | Grows per year | Breeds |
|---|---|---|
| ⚔️ Martial | +0.4 strength, +0.5 combat, +0.2 leadership | Brave |
| 💰 Stewardship | +0.25 intelligence, +0.1 luck, +0.5 commerce, +0.25 leadership | Honest, Greedy |
| 🤝 Diplomacy | +0.4 charisma, +0.5 diplomacy, +0.15 leadership | Generous, Honest |
| 📜 Learning | +0.45 intelligence, +0.4 cartography, +0.2 diplomacy, +0.1 commerce | Scholar |
| 🦊 Intrigue | +0.2 intelligence, +0.2 luck, +0.5 stealth, +0.15 diplomacy | Cunning (a little Deceitful) |
| 🙏 Piety | +0.45 faith, +0.1 charisma, +0.25 diplomacy, +0.1 leadership | Pious, Merciful |

Stats cap at 14 and skills at 8 during childhood.

**Tutors.** A tutor multiplies the growth. The full bonus applies when their subject matches the focus; otherwise only 30% of it does.

- *Court tutors* cost gold up front plus a monthly wage, paid every 30 days. An unpaid tutor leaves. The Hedge Tutor teaches all subjects (×1.25, 40g + 6g a month). The Master-at-Arms, Retired Steward, Court Envoy, Court Scholar, Whisperer and House Chaplain each teach one focus (×1.8–1.9, 120–180g + 14–22g a month). Each also passes on some of their own character: the Master-at-Arms breeds bravery, the Whisperer cunning.
- *A companion* travelling with you teaches for free. Their occupation decides the subject: knights and guards teach martial arts, merchants stewardship, scholars and healers learning, priests piety, and so on. Their mood and their friendships in the party scale the bonus, re-checked every month: a content companion teaches at ×1.7 when they have a subject (×1.2 otherwise), a loyal or devoted one better still. A disgruntled or mutinous companion refuses to teach, and stops if their mood sours. Their values rub off: an honourable companion breeds honesty, a greedy one greed. They stop teaching when they leave your party.

**Childhood events.** Every 40 days, each child aged 6–15 has a 45% chance of a dilemma for you to decide. The dilemmas are a bully, a fever, a prodigy moment, a scandal, a duel challenge, a religious calling, the first hunt, a beggar at the gate, the locked library, dice in the stables, the children's court, dreams of the sea, a sibling feud, a wandering mentor, a lie, and a harvest feast. Each choice shapes the child's skills, stats and character. Some choices cost gold and some are risky (⚠️): a fever left to run its course can weaken a child for life, or rarely kill them. Duels and hunts are contests of strength and combat skill. An event left unanswered for 20 days resolves itself with the household's default choice.

**Character.** A child's personality grows from four sources: the focus, the tutor, your choices in their dilemmas, and **your example**. Each of your own earned traits has a 35% chance a year to rub off on each child. Once a tendency is strong enough it shows as a **childhood trait**. A child holds up to three, and of two opposites only the stronger counts. The panel also shows the traits still forming.

**Coming of age (16).** The child's childhood traits become fixed **adult traits**, their tutor is released, and the House chronicle records it. A grown child can then:

- hold a **House office** (Steward, Marshal, Chancellor, Spymaster, Heir), which uses their skills;
- make an **alliance match** into a kingdom's nobility: a 200 gold dowry, for +15 reputation with that kingdom and +4 Prestige;
- make a **royal match** with an unwed prince or princess: 500 gold and 20 Prestige (needs 20 reputation), for +20 reputation and a claim of 15 on that throne;
- make a **match in town** with one of the townsfolk of the right sex, unwed and within 12 years of their age, from any town whose people you have walked among (people you have spoken with are listed first). There is no dowry. It costs 2 Prestige for marrying beneath the House, or gains 1 if the match is from a noble or merchant family. You gain +4 reputation with the town's realm, and the match's parents and siblings think 15 points better of you. The townsperson leaves home to live with the House (the town remembers: *"Wren Millbrook wed into House Vale in the spring of 861. Imagine that."*). If that child inherits, the townsperson becomes the new ruler's spouse;
- be sent as a **ward** to a ruler's court for 120 days. A ward cannot hold an office while away. They return with +1 leadership, +1 diplomacy, +10 reputation with that realm, and often one of the host's traits: a devout ruler teaches piety, a cunning one cunning. Each child can be fostered once.

Married and fostered children stay in the line of succession.

**Succession.** The heir takes over with their own stats and skills, and their adult traits become real earned traits. A regency heir keeps the childhood traits they had. Like any earned trait, these can later be eroded by contrary deeds.

#### Playing the Heir Before They Inherit

What you do with your children while you live pays off when one of them succeeds you (`systems/heirAgency.js`, tuned in `data/upbringing.json` → `agency`). Open a child's page (**Dynasty → 📖**). The Dynasty list shows where each child is and what they are doing.

**Fostering a child (ages 7–15).** Send the child to be raised at a ruler's court for 1, 2 or 3 years. You cannot foster at a court you are at war with, or one where your reputation is below −10.
- The host's court becomes the child's tutor at ×1.4. Its subject follows the realm's temper: martial in warlike realms, stewardship in mercantile ones, diplomacy elsewhere. Any tutor at home is let go.
- The host ruler's character seeds the child's own. They come home with it as a childhood trait, fixed at 16.
- A **bond** with that court grows by 1 a month, up to 20.
- Fostering a child at a **rival House's** court cools that feud by 10.
- If the court comes to think ill of you (its regard falls to −30 in peacetime), it keeps the child as a **hostage**. A held child does not come home. Ransom them home by letter (Post); a fosterage cut short this way counts for nothing at succession. At open war the danger comes by letter instead: the child writes that they are afraid.
- A grown child goes as a ward for a season instead (see above).

**Betrothal (ages 4–15).** Promise a child to a realm's nobility. It costs half the alliance dowry (100 gold) and gives +8 reputation, and the lord remembers it. When the child comes of age the match is made, with the rest of the alliance's standing and Prestige. Breaking a betrothal costs 15 reputation and a lasting grudge. If the child is married elsewhere, the betrothal breaks itself.

**Kin are slow to turn on you.** A realm bound to your House by marriage (yours, or a child wed into it) counts only half its hostility toward a league against you. A realm bound by a betrothal counts three-quarters (see [Coalitions & Threats](#coalitions--threats)). The Threats panel shows the reason.

**Commissions for a grown child.** A grown child at home can take one commission with real stakes:
- **Command the company.** The child leads your army. Battle power rises by 2.5% per point of their combat and 1.5% per point of leadership, up to +30%. Every battle teaches them combat (+0.5) and leadership (+0.25). Every battle also risks them:
  - in a victory: 6% chance of a wound, 1% of death;
  - in a defeat: 20% wound, 8% capture, 4% death.

  A wounded child rests 30 days. A captive's captors ask 150 gold, paid from the child's page. Unpaid, they let the child go after 60 days, and the House loses 6 Prestige for not paying for its own. A death is final. Fate is rolled from the world seed, so a reload does not change it.
- **Envoy.** The child spends 30 days at a realm's court (40 gold). They come home with +6 reputation there (+1.5 more per point of their diplomacy), the lord's memory of the embassy, a bond (+4) and +1 diplomacy. If your realms go to war meanwhile, the court keeps them as a hostage.
- **Steward.** Appoint the child governor of a holding from that holding's panel. Every 90 days in office teaches +1 commerce, and the days count toward their record.

The child's outcomes come home as letters (✒️ in **Letters**). Writing back warms the court they wrote of.

**What the record carries into a reign.** The child's page previews it under *Should they inherit*. When they succeed:

| Record | Payoff |
|---|---|
| Fostered at a court (not recalled) | That lord remembers *"I raised the one who rules your House now"* (14 + 0.6 × bond, fading over ~1,500 days), +10 reputation there, +4 Prestige |
| Envoy to a court | +3 reputation there, +1 Prestige per realm |
| Battles commanded | +2 Prestige (up to 15) and +5 renown (up to 50) per battle won; 3+ victories make the new ruler **Brave** |
| Taken captive | −3 Prestige each time |
| Governed a holding 180+ days | +1 commerce, +3 Prestige |
| Wed into a realm | Its lord counts the throne as kin (marriage memory), +3 Prestige |

The new reign's chapter of the **Annals** opens with this life (*"Corin came to the seat with a life already lived: raised at the court of Maud of Arvenland; commanded the House's company in 3 battles…"*).

#### A Regency

When the heir is under 16, you may install a regency (`systems/regency.js`, tuned in `data/upbringing.json` → `regency`). The child keeps their real age, and you play the child's court until they come of age. The House pays the old price too: a regency sheds holdings (the title and a tenth of the gold).

**The regent.** On the death screen, choose who rules in the child's name:
- the late ruler's **spouse** (the child's parent): loyal (72), seldom ambitious;
- a **grown sibling** at home: less loyal (58), often ambitious;
- a lord of the **regency council** (55).

Ambition varies with the person and their traits. Every week the regent's loyalty drifts toward what the House can command: their kind, your Prestige, favours you have shown, less their ambition.
- A regent below 45 loyalty skims the treasury, up to 120 gold a week. It shows in the Ledger under *The regency*, and your steward reports the shortfall each month.
- A regent below 25 loyalty with ambition 50+ **plots**. Whispers warn you once the plot is half-grown. When it ripens they stage a **coup**: a quarter of your gold, then your office and a tenth of what is left, and 10 Prestige.
- If the regent dies, a council lord takes over.

**The child's court** (**Dynasty → 👑 Regency**):
- **A gift** (100 gold) wins +8 loyalty.
- **Public honours** (8 Prestige) win +12 loyalty and cool their ambition.
- **Put the regent aside** for a council lord (15 Prestige). It costs only 5 Prestige once a plot has been exposed, and then half of what they skimmed is recovered. Putting a sibling aside without cause leaves them nursing a claim on the next succession.
- **Assert your majority** from age 14 (12 Prestige).

**Limits of a child ruler.** No war may be declared and no siege laid while a regent rules. In battle the company fights under the regent's captains (×0.92), unless a sibling of the blood commands it.

**Coming of age.** At 16 (or when you assert it) the regency ends and you decide what becomes of the regent's men:
- **Keep them.** A loyal regent's men are steady hands: +1 diplomacy, +10 renown. A disloyal regent's men take one last cut.
- **Purge them.** Recover half of what was skimmed. Gain 6 Prestige if they plotted, otherwise lose 5 for ingratitude. A purged sibling nurses a claim.
- **Reward them** (200 gold): +6 Prestige, and every House officer's loyalty rises by 10.

Undecided after 14 days, the men stay. The regency's start, its turns and its end are written in the House Chronicle and the Annals.

### The House Chronicle and Legacy

**Dynasty → 📜 House Chronicle** tells your House's story one ruler at a time. Each reign shows:

- its years, the ruler's age and cause of death, and their earned traits;
- any title or crown held;
- what happened in it: crowns won (taken or founded), settlements taken, battles won (war hosts, raiders, pirates, monsters and saga beasts), wars fought, quests, titles gained, delves cleared, rival adventurers bested (duels, races, rivals slain), petitions ruled (with the notable rulings), children born, ambition milestones, holding projects built, raids repelled, rebellions crushed, holdings lost and Prestige gained;
- the chronicle's most important entries.

Each ruler also earns an epithet from their deeds ("the Crowned", "the Conqueror", "the Warlike", "the Delver", "the Duellist", "the Judge"), or else from their first trait ("the Brave").

**Legacy score.** Each reign scores:

| Deed | Points |
|---|---|
| Year ruled | 2 |
| Crown won | 150 |
| Ambition fulfilled | 60 |
| Settlement taken | 25 |
| Title gained | 20 |
| Ambition milestone, noble marriage | 10 each |
| Delve cleared | 6 |
| Rival bested, child born | 5 each |
| Earned trait | 4 |
| Rebellion crushed | 6 |
| Raid repelled | 4 |
| Battle won, holding project built | 3 each |
| Quest | 2 |
| Petition ruled | 1 |

The House adds 40 per generation and 1 per point of Prestige.

### Deeds & Records

**Journal → 🏅 Deeds & Records** (also the button under your name on the **Character Sheet**) gathers every record the realm keeps on you into one ledger, grouped by system. Two columns: **House**, the lifetime total of your line, and **Reign**, the current ruler's share since they took the mantle. Present-tense facts (holdings ruled, claims held, your hunter rank, guild rank, trading posts, company capital, companions travelling with you) show as "now". A section with nothing in it yet is folded into a "No record yet" line.

| Section | Records |
|---|---|
| ⚔️ Arms & House | battles won, quests, titles, settlements taken, noble marriages, ambition milestones |
| 👑 The Throne | crowns won, claims held, succession crises and civil wars underway, reigning, throne chronicle entries |
| 🏛️ The Royal Court | vassal lords and edicts in force; edicts proclaimed, royal feasts, grants and favours, plots uncovered, revolts (crushed and conceded), lords broken, crown monopoly revenue, grain given in the famine dole, crowns lost or set down, days ruled under them. These tallies include every crown your House has worn and lost, so they don't reset when a crown goes |
| 🏯 Holdings Besieged | under siege now; sieges endured, relieved, held, saved by terms, bought off, lifted by peace, fallen |
| 🏰 Holdings | holdings ruled, projects built, raids repelled and suffered, rebellions and rebellions crushed, holdings lost, governors who betrayed you |
| ⚖️ Petitions & Court | petitions ruled and ignored |
| 🛡️ Sieges | towns besieged, taken, sacked, terms accepted, sieges lifted and broken, relief armies beaten |
| 📜 Sagas | sagas begun, joined, won and ended; saga beasts slain and their bounty gold |
| 🎯 Bounty Hunting | hunter rank, bounties completed, outlaws turned in or recruited, captures botched, saga beasts on notice |
| 🕳️ Delves | delves entered, chambers explored, delves cleared, deepest chamber, retreats, collapses |
| 🗡️ Rival Adventurers | duels and races won and lost, rivals slain and sabotaged, partnerships, hires |
| 🤝 Companions | travelling with you, devoted, fallen, deserted, betrayed you |
| 🌑 The Underworld | guild rank, crimes, times caught, gold from crime |
| 🏦 Trading Company | posts, capital, contracts fulfilled and failed, posts robbed, convoys lost |
| 🧭 Discoveries | lands found and named, exotic hauls, charts sold, colonies, naval contests, lands rivals found first |
| ⛵ Voyages | voyages completed, days at sea, events at sea, longest voyage |
| 🩹 Wounds & Sickness | wounds, diseases, recoveries, festered wounds, scars, treatments |
| 📯 The Heralds | news proclaimed in the world and proclamations you heard, with the last five that reached you (including any the heralds' daily limit kept quiet) |
| 🌿 Living off the Land | foraging trips and hunts, with your forager's and hunter's craft rank |
| 🕯️ Whisper Campaigns | tales planted, fanned and quashed, lies believed, your lies debunked, whispers traced to you, rulers blackmailed, proofs sold |
| 🗡️ Notoriety | killings (witnessed and unseen); bounties on your head and the gold on it now |
| 🌾 Estates & Land | estates bought and sold, gold paid and received, rents banked, arrears collected, times you pressed for them, improvements, enclosures, dowries, confiscations |
| 🧶 The Putting-Out Trade | units put out, cloth collected, piece wages, guild fines, units seized |
| 💰 Money & Trade | gold drawn by bills of exchange, gold got by forged bills and forgeries caught, gold put into crown bonds and coupons earned, shares bought back, forward contracts delivered and failed, trade laws breached, goods smuggled, times caught |
| 📒 The Realms' Books | (the world's, "now") grain engrossed, sold back, spoiled, edicts against engrossers and grain thrown open; tax farms sold (and those you bought); tax revolts |

Below the sections, the ledger lists your **achievements** by tier, each with the day it was won, and the name of every quest you completed.

**A lost crown's record.** When you abdicate, or your realm falls, the reign's royal-court record goes into the House's crown ledger: the realm, the days ruled, how the reign ended, the edicts in force, the lords and every court tally. A line is also added to the House chronicle. In the House Chronicle, each ruler's card shows the court they kept (edicts, feasts, plots uncovered, revolts crushed and conceded, lords broken, monopoly revenue, dole grain) and any crown they lost. The ledger is saved with the House.

A reign that began in a save from before the ledger existed is counted from the House's beginning. The voyage record is saved with your character.

**The line ends.** If you die with no heir, or let the dynasty end rather than install a regency, the epitaph screen shows your House's sigil and motto, its generations, years and crowns, every reign, and its rank:

| Score | Rank |
|---|---|
| Under 150 | A Forgotten Line |
| 150+ | A Minor House |
| 400+ | A Respected House |
| 900+ | A Great House |
| 1,800+ | A Legendary Dynasty |

### The Annals: Your Story as a Book

**📖 The Annals** (Journal → The Annals, Dynasty → 📖 The Annals, the death screen's *Read the Annals of your life*, the Jump In panel while time passes, and the welcome of a new life) tell your life and your dynasty as a chronicle you can read back. Nothing new is tracked for it: the book is written, whenever you open it, from what the game already keeps — the House chronicle, each ruler's reign record, your traits, the sagas you took part in, your pilgrimages, the bards' honours (gold and legendary achievements), the money ledger, your fame in each realm, and what the lords remember of you.

**Chapters.** Each ruler of the House has a chapter, titled from their standing and the name the realm gave them — *The Reign of Aldric the Brave, 1st of House Ashford, years 1012–1031* for a crowned ruler, *The Lordship of…* for one with offices or towns, *The Life of…* otherwise. A chapter opens with how they came to the House (founding it, inheriting it — disputed or under a regency — or, for a new life, where they came from), then tells the notable years in order, season by season. Only what matters makes the book: births, marriages and widowhood, wars, sieges and conquests, crowns won and lost, offices, Great Works, discoveries and sagas outweigh a guild's demand or a rival's grumble; a busy year keeps its six weightiest events and notes the rest, and long quiet stretches are passed over in a line. Each chapter closes with its themes: **war** (battles, towns taken, sieges, beasts of legend), **rise** (crowns, offices, Prestige, judgements remembered, quests), **wealth** (the purse when the ruler took it and left it, the peak and the lean years, and — for the living — where the coin comes from and goes), **love & family** (the spouse, the children born), **faith** (prayers, pilgrimages, vows kept and broken, a faith founded), **infamy** (lives taken, crimes, whispers, rivals bested, a dark name), and **death** — the cause, the age and the name they would be remembered by. The living ruler's chapter ends *to the present*.

**Legacy.** The last page measures the House: its rank and Legacy score, its Prestige, its greatest ruler, what it holds now, its fortune from first to peak to today, where its name is celebrated and where it is cursed, the enemies it has made (rival Houses with a deep feud, lords who remember you ill) and its friends, and the children of its blood who live.

**Before.** When you begin a new life as someone else, the old line's book goes with you: it is shown under *Before: House …* tabs, and the new House starts a book of its own. An heir's book simply goes on: every earlier ruler keeps their chapter.

**Sharing.** *📋 Copy as text* puts the whole book (with any *Before* books) on the clipboard; if the browser will not allow it, the text is shown ready to select.

**The court scribe.** With the local language model switched on (Settings → *Enable Local LLM NPCs*), each chapter offers *✒️ Have the court scribe write this chapter*: the model retells the chapter as flowing prose, using only the chapter's own facts. It takes a moment and never holds up the game; if the model is off, slow or unreachable, the annals simply stand as written. You can switch between the scribe's chapter and the plain annals.

The book is told in the past tense: the House chronicle's lines (*"Bran weds Tamsin"*) are put into the past (*"Bran wed Tamsin"*) as they enter it; titles in quotes are left as written. The phrasing is chosen by the event and its day, never by chance, so the same game always reads the same. The wealth curve is a mark of the purse each season, kept with the House (older marks are thinned so a long dynasty keeps its whole curve). Phrases and what counts as notable are in `data/annals.json`.

---

## 19. Culture

### Cultural Buildings

| Building | Cost | Build Time | Effect (level 1) |
|----------|------|-----------|--------|
| Scriptorium | 400g | 4 days | +10% research speed, 1 gold/day |
| Library | 600g | 6 days | +15% research speed, 1.5 gold/day |
| Theater | 800g | 8 days | +2% army morale, 2.5 gold/day |
| Monument | 1,000g | 10 days | +1 renown/day, 2 gold/day |
| University | 1,500g | 14 days | +30% research speed, 4 gold/day (requires 1,000 pop) |

Effects start once construction finishes. **Research speed** from Scriptoria, Libraries and Universities adds up (capped at +50%) and shortens every technology you start researching, on top of INT, Writing/Printing Press and University properties. **Income** is a tenth of the building's culture rating per level per day (Philosophy and Printing Press multiply it); fractions carry over, so a Library pays 3 gold every 2 days. Theaters' morale bonus is capped at +6% across all of them.

**Upgrades.** Stand on one of your finished cultural buildings and choose **Upgrade** (3 AP) to raise it to level 2 for half its build cost, then to level 3 for its full build cost (a Library: 300g, then 600g). Each level multiplies income, renown and influence by the level (×2, ×3) and adds +50% to its research and morale bonus (×1.5, ×2). The hex info panel and the Culture overview show each building's level, real income and upgrade price.

### Cultural Traditions

Each kingdom's culture provides distinct bonuses:
- **Imperial**: +military, +diplomacy, +trade
- **Woodland**: +nature, +stealth, +archery
- **Nomadic**: +cavalry, +endurance, +raiding
- **Maritime**: +trade, +navigation
- **Mountain**: +mining, +defense, +craftsmanship
- **Nordic**: +naval, +raiding, +endurance
- **Desert**: +trade, +endurance, +hospitality
- **Swamp**: +alchemy, +stealth, +poison
- **Arcane**: +scholarship, +research, +magic

These are real kingdom bonuses (shown in the kingdom info panel and Cultural overview): military/cavalry/archery/naval add army strength (cavalry and archery also shift the army's mix), raiding adds battle power, defense helps defenders, endurance resists attrition, trade/navigation/hospitality open trade routes, scholarship/research/magic raise scholarship, faith raises piety, mining adds treasury income, craftsmanship speeds construction, nature/alchemy help population growth, and stealth/poison add intrigue against rebels.

### Scholarship & Influence

Every kingdom has **Scholarship** and **Influence** (0–100). They grow slowly on their own, faster with scholarly traditions and rulers, and much faster with cultural buildings. They have real effects:

- **Scholarship** → up to +5% kingdom income (learned stewards) and +20% construction (repairs and founding villages).
- **Influence** → up to +3 relations per diplomatic envoy, and *cultural pull*: a realm's envoys warm by +1 per 20 points of influence the other realm has over it (max +3), so admired cultures make friends and fewer enemies.
- **Both** → stability: ±0.1 per point that their average sits above or below 40 (from −4 to +6).

The kingdom info panel and the Culture overview list each realm's current culture effects.

### Kingdom Cultural Buildings

Kingdoms commission their own cultural buildings (about once every three months, at most 3 per realm, one per town) when the treasury holds at least three times the cost. They are built over the usual construction time, then each adds 5% of its scholarship rating and 10% of its influence rating per day (a Library: +0.25 scholarship, +0.2 influence). If the town is conquered, the building passes to the conqueror.

### Cultural Events

13 event types can occur (about 1% per kingdom per day, paid from its treasury, lasting 3–7 days). While an event runs, its effect is real:

| Effect | Events | While running |
|--------|--------|---------------|
| Morale | Grand Festival, Great Horse Race, Harvest Feast, Gladiatorial Games | +0.2 stability target per point, +value/200 battle morale |
| Scholarship / Research | Book Fair, Philosophical Debate, Arcane Symposium | +value/10 scholarship per day |
| Influence | Art Exhibition | +value/10 influence per day |
| Renown | Bardic Competition | +value/40 diplomacy (+2 relations per envoy) |
| Industry | Forge Festival | +value% construction, +value/2 % income |
| Trade | Naval Regatta, Caravan Festival | +value% trade route chance, +value/2 % income |
| Faith | Spirit Walk | +value/10 piety per day |

---

## 20. Colonization

### Founding Colonies

You can found colonies on unclaimed wilderness (costs gold). Recruit settlers from other settlements to grow your colony's population.

### Indigenous Tribes

7 indigenous groups inhabit the world, each with different hostility and tradeability levels:

| Tribe | Terrain | Hostility | Trade Openness |
|-------|---------|-----------|---------------|
| Forest Folk | Forests | Low-Medium | High |
| Steppe Riders | Plains, Savanna | Medium | Medium |
| Mountain Clans | Mountains, Hills | Medium | Low |
| Desert Nomads | Deserts | Low | Very High |
| Swamp Dwellers | Swamps | High | Very Low |
| Coastal Tribes | Coast, Beach | Very Low | Very High |
| Tundra People | Tundra, Snow | Low | Low |

**Landfall colonies.** A colony can also be founded from a ship's landing party on unclaimed coast: cheaper, with settlers and stores from the hold, but without a quay until you build a jetty (see [Landing Parties](#landing-parties)).

**Uncharted lands.** A colony founded on or within 3 hexes of an island, cove or valley you found claims it. You alone gather its exotic goods, and more of them. Realms that know of the place may contest the claim with their navies (see [Uncharted Lands](#uncharted-lands)).

### Colonization Policies

| Policy | Expansion | Indigenous Relations | Loyalty | Production |
|--------|-----------|---------------------|---------|-----------|
| Manifest Destiny | 1.5× | Very Negative | +5% | +10% |
| Coexistence | 0.8× | Very Positive | +15% | No bonus |
| Exploitation | 1.0× | Very Negative | −10% | +30% |
| Missionary | 1.0× | Slightly Positive | +10% | +5% |

**Missionary work.** A Missionary colony befriends tribes within 4 hexes (+0.05 relation a day) and converts them to its faith. If it is your colony and you have founded a religion, it spreads *your* faith: every settlement within 4 hexes gains a share of followers each day, and converted tribesfolk join your faith (2% of a tribe's people a day). The mother kingdom gains cultural influence and piety (+0.01 a day per colony, at most +0.05). The colony panel shows how far its missionaries reach. Religious kingdoms send Missionary colonies of their own.

### Developing Your Holdings

Your **holdings** are the settlements you rule and tax: colonies you founded and towns you conquered that are still yours. Open **🏰 Manage Holding** from the action menu at one of them (or from the colony panel). The panel shows the town's development slots, the projects being built, its governor and garrison, and a summary of its numbers: weekly tax (and how much the governor skims), happiness and the target it is drifting toward, loyalty, unrest, defence against the rebels it could raise, garrison strength and upkeep, days of food in store and yearly growth.

**Projects.** A holding has a few development slots, and more as it grows:

| Tier | Population | Slots |
|------|-----------|-------|
| Hamlet | under 150 | 2 |
| Village | 150+ | 3 |
| Town | 600+ | 4 |
| City | 2,000+ | 5 |
| Great City | 5,000+ | 6 |

A project costs gold, materials and days. Materials come out of your packs. Anything you lack is bought on the spot at 1.5× its price (wood and stone 12g, iron 30g, tools 52g). Stone and timber near the town shorten the work. A good steward speeds it up (+6% per point of stewardship). You can abandon a project under construction for half its gold back. The panel shows each project's effect before you build it, with the gold it would add to the weekly tax and the change in defence.

| Project | Cost | Days | Effect |
|---------|------|------|--------|
| 🚧 Palisade | 150g, 20 wood | 6 | +25% defence, 20% fewer bandit raids, calms unrest |
| 🏰 Stone Walls (replaces the Palisade, village+) | 600g, 40 stone, 4 tools | 15 | +60% defence, 45% fewer raids, calms unrest more |
| 🏪 Market Square | 300g, 10 wood, 15 stone | 8 | +12% tax, +4% on what you sell there, +2 happiness, +1% growth a year |
| 🌾 Granary | 200g, 25 wood | 6 | +6 days of food in store (the lean season), +1% growth, +1 happiness |
| 🕯️ Shrine | 120g, 8 stone | 4 | +3 happiness, spreads your faith there (0.4% of the unbelievers a day), calms unrest |
| ⛪ Temple (replaces the Shrine, village+) | 550g, 35 stone, 10 wood | 12 | +6 happiness, spreads your faith (1% a day), calms unrest more |
| ⚔️ Barracks | 350g, 20 wood, 8 iron | 9 | +10 militia defence, +50% recruits a day and 10% cheaper recruits here, garrison upkeep −25% |
| ⚓ Harbour (beside the water only) | 450g, 35 wood, 10 stone | 12 | +8% tax, 8% better port-market prices for you, free repairs (+5% a day) for your ships docked there, +2 days of food, +1.5% growth |
| 🗼 Watchtower | 180g, 10 wood, 10 stone | 5 | 40% fewer raids, your underworld heat in the town's realm (for an independent town, the nearest realm) cools 0.5 a day faster, +5% defence |
| 🔨 Workshop Quarter (village+) | 400g, 20 wood, 10 iron, 4 tools | 10 | +20% goods produced, +6% tax |
| 🍺 Tavern and Inn | 250g, 20 wood | 7 | +4 happiness, +1.5% growth, +10% daily chance of settlers arriving at a young colony, +3% tax |
| 🏛️ Moot Hall (village+) | 320g, 15 stone, 10 wood | 8 | petitioners from the town wait 4 days longer, +2 happiness, calms unrest most |

A **governor** also keeps petitioners in hand while you are away: petitions from a governed town wait 1 extra day per 3 points of the governor's Stewardship (up to 3).

Happiness from projects raises the target the town drifts toward at each tax collection. Growth adds to the tax policy's yearly growth, and in a kingdom's colony it also speeds the daily growth. Walls also stand if the town is ever taken from you, so retaking it will be harder.

**Governor.** Appoint a governor from your travelling companions (they leave your party until relieved, and cannot be invited back while in office; a companion's personal quest waits for them), your spouse (who also leaves your party if travelling with you) or a child aged 16 or over, or a steward for hire (100g, then 3–6g a day; three are on offer each month). Each has **Stewardship**, **Martial** and **Intrigue** from 0 to 10:

- **Stewardship:** +3% tax and +0.3% growth a year per point, and faster building.
- **Martial:** +4% defence per point, and calms unrest.
- **Intrigue:** calms unrest and wards off raids (+2% per point), but makes a disloyal governor skim more.

A governor's **loyalty** settles toward a base (family 80, a companion's approval of you, a steward 55), plus up to +10 from your renown and ±5 from the town's mood, minus 2 per point of intrigue (ambition) and 15 while their wages go unpaid. Below 70 loyalty a governor **skims** the taxes: up to half of them, more the craftier they are. Below 20 loyalty they may **betray you**, fleeing with up to 10% of your gold (at most 300g) and leaving the town in uproar (+40 unrest). You are warned when a governor grows restless.

**Neglect.** A holding with no governor drifts while you are more than 6 hexes away. Each day it loses 0.25 happiness (down to 20) and 0.15 loyalty, and its unrest grows.

**Garrison.** At the holding you can station soldiers from your army there, and march them out again. Hired mercenaries and warships can't be stationed. The garrison adds its strength to the town's defence against kingdom war hosts, bandits and rebels. It also calms unrest (fully once it reaches 2% of the population, at least 20 strength), and it is paid from your purse every day at the troops' normal upkeep. If you can't pay, a tenth of it deserts. In a siege (see [Holdings Under Siege](#holdings-under-siege)) the garrison's losses fall on the stationed troops. If the town is lost, its garrison is lost with it.

**Defence** = (the colony's own guard, or 10) + 1% of the population + garrison strength + militia, × (1 + walls, watchtower and governor bonuses).

**Unrest and rebellion.** Unrest (0–100) eases by 0.25 a day on its own and is raised by:
- unhappiness below 40, up to +1.5 a day
- loyalty below 30, up to +0.8 a day
- high taxes (+0.15 a day, very high +0.35)
- neglect (+0.4 a day)
- crime and idle hands (crime × 0.4, plus 2 × the share of hands idle beyond 6% — see [Labour, Classes and Migration](#labour-classes-and-migration))

It is calmed by order from projects and the governor, by your troops, and by your presence (−0.5 a day while you are within a hex). From 60 you are warned. From 75, a holding whose garrison couldn't beat its rebels may rise in revolt (rebels number 10 + 4% of the population). At 100 it rises for certain. A strong enough garrison puts the riots down instead, losing some troops.

A **rebellion** stops all taxes and building there. A garrison that grows strong enough crushes it on its own. Otherwise you have **15 days**:
- **Crush the rebels** with your army at the holding (a battle).
- **Buy them off** for half a gold coin per inhabitant (at least 100g) and 5 renown.

If the rebels still hold the town at the deadline, it becomes a free town and is no longer yours.

**Bandit raids.** Raider bands within 2 hexes of a holding may fall on it (about 6% a day per band, less with walls, a watchtower and a crafty governor, and at most once a week). If the town's defence beats the band, the band is destroyed. Otherwise the raiders burn 30% of its stored food (and 15% of the market's stores, which damages any works there), kill 3% of its people, cost 6 happiness and 8 unrest, and set back construction by 2 days.

**Sea reavers.** A coastal holding near a pirate captain's haven may also be raided from the sea (see [Landing Parties](#landing-parties)). The same defence keeps them out.

A holding you lose — to rebels or a war host — keeps only its walls. Its harbour, markets, workshops and other works stop serving you until you take it back.

Projects, governors, garrisons, unrest and rebellions are all saved with the game.

---

## Patronage & Great Works

Gold piles up; Patronage is where it goes to last. You can keep **clients** (artists, scholars, architects, physicians and chroniclers) who make works in your name, and raise **Great Works**: cathedrals, libraries and wonders that stand on the map for good and change how other parts of the game play. Open it from **Patronage & Great Works** in a town's action menu (the 🎨 social action), or from the Counsel.

### Clients

Every **town and capital** keeps a **salon** of three people looking for a patron. The salon changes each month, and the same town offers the same faces to anyone in the same month. Each candidate has:

| | |
|---|---|
| **Profession** | 🎨 Artist (paintings, sculptures) · 📚 Scholar (treatises) · 📐 Architect (oversees a Great Work) · ⚕️ Physician (medical texts) · 📜 Chronicler (chronicles of your House) |
| **Skill** (1–10) | How fast they work and how good the work is. Capitals draw better talent. |
| **Temperament** | Diligent (fast, frets when idle) · Mercurial (slow, touched by genius) · Proud (costly, loyal to a great House) · Devoted (cheap and faithful) · Restless (quick, with a wandering eye) |
| **Ambition** | Fame (wants works donated where people see them) · Wealth (wants a fat stipend, easy to poach) · Mastery (wants commissions, gains skill with each work) · Glory (wants to serve a House of 150+ Prestige) |
| **Stipend** | Gold a week. Hiring costs two weeks up front. |

You can keep **4 clients** (5 while you hold a royal office). Stipends are paid every 7 days. A client who goes **unpaid three weeks** leaves, and so does one whose **contentment** falls near zero. Contentment rises while they work and are paid, falls while they sit **idle** (after a two-week grace), and moves each month with their ambition. You can **raise a stipend** by a fifth to cheer someone up.

**Commissions.** Set an idle client to work. The materials come out of your packs, and anything missing is bought for you at a markup. Each work comes out **modest**, **fine** or a **masterpiece**: skill, temperament and contentment decide which, and a masterpiece counts for about 1.6×.

| Work | Takes | Materials | Effect |
|------|-------|-----------|--------|
| 🖼️ Painting | ~35 days | 2 dyes | +4 Prestige; goes to your **gallery** |
| 🗿 Sculpture | ~55 days | 8 stone | +6 Prestige; goes to your gallery |
| 📖 Treatise | ~40 days | 2 parchment | 6 days of progress on your **current research**. Days it can't use are banked and fed into later research one day at a time. |
| ⚕️ Medical text | ~45 days | 4 herbs, 1 parchment | Sickness is less likely to worsen or kill (×0.94 per text, down to ×0.6), and wounds mend faster (+0.1 a day per text, up to +0.5) |
| 📜 Chronicle | ~60 days | 3 parchment | +8 Prestige, an entry in the House chronicle, and +4 to the reign's **Legacy** score |

**The gallery.** Keep paintings and sculptures, or **donate** one to the town you stand in. A town you rule grows happier: its happiness target rises by 1.5 per point of art, up to +8. A town elsewhere raises your standing with its realm by 3 per point. Each donation also gives +1 renown, and a client who seeks fame is glad of it.

**Rival Houses poach.** Every couple of weeks a rival House (see *The House*) may court one of your clients. The chance is higher for skilled, restless or wealth-seeking clients, for unhappy ones, and when the feud with that House runs hot. You have **6 days** to **match the offer** (about ten weeks' stipend; the client stays on 25% more pay and is happier for it) or **let them go**. If you don't answer, the client decides alone, and a contented client usually stays. Every client lost to a rival inflames the feud.

### Great Works

A Great Work rises at a settlement **you rule** (a holding) or one where **you own a house** (your seat). You can raise up to two at once, one per town, and each kind only once.

| Work | Needs | Stages | Grants (while the town is yours) |
|------|-------|--------|----------------------------------|
| ⛪ Grand Cathedral | town | 3 | Your faith converts +25% faster; town happiness +6 |
| 🏛️ The Great Library *(unique)* | town | 3 | Research +20% faster; happiness +2 |
| 🎓 University | town | 2 | The heirs of your House learn 35% faster; happiness +2 |
| 🔭 The Royal Observatory *(unique)* | village | 2 | You see 1 hex further everywhere |
| 🏟️ Hippodrome | town | 2 | +4 renown from every festival you host; happiness +5 |
| 🌉 Grand Bridge | a river | 2 | Fording rivers costs half the effort, everywhere |
| 🗽 The Colossus *(unique)* | a harbour | 3 | +6 House Prestige every month; happiness +3 |

Finishing one also gives a large one-time Prestige award (40–100), renown, and a line in your House chronicle. It also scores **+40** on the reign's Legacy.

**Building.** Each stage wants **goods** (stone, timber, iron, glass, parchment…) and **gold** delivered first. Goods in your packs can only be handed over **on site**. From anywhere you can have the rest **bought for you**, at a markup that is steeper when you're away. Once a stage is supplied, the masons work it for a set number of days, and their **wages** (6–12 gold a day) come out of your purse each day. Work goes faster in bigger towns and with an **architect** in charge (+5% per point of skill). It **slows by half** when the town's realm, or you, are at war. It **stops** in:

- **winter**, for outdoor stages (foundations, walls, statues; interiors carry on)
- a **famine** in the region
- a **siege** of the town
- when the purse **can't pay** the day's wages
- when the town is **no longer yours**

A finished work stands on the map with a gold ring. Its gifts last only while you hold the town, but they come back if you retake it.

### The race for wonders

Kingdoms build too. From about day 90 a realm with a full treasury may start a wonder at its capital and pay for it day by day. Winter and war slow it, and it stalls when the treasury runs dry. A finished wonder steadies the realm (its stability target rises). **Unique** works (the Great Library, the Royal Observatory, the Colossus) exist once in the world: **the first to finish claims it**. If a kingdom finishes yours first, your half-built works are sold off for half of what you spent. If you finish first, the kingdoms racing you give up. The heralds proclaim every wonder begun and finished, and townsfolk talk about them. The **Wonders** tab of the panel and the Counsel show how far each rival has got.

**Fashion at Court** (👗 tab): standing in a realm's capital where you have standing, you can set the court's fashion for a season — see [The Economic Chronicle](#the-economic-chronicle-the-gazette-your-books-and-fashions).

**The Counsel** warns of rival offers, stalled works and races you might lose, and reminds you of idle clients and stages waiting for supplies. **Achievements:** Patron of the Arts, A Masterpiece, Great Builder, Wonder of the World, A Golden Age. **Deeds & Records** has a Patronage section.

---

## 21. Tavern & Intelligence

Visit taverns in cities and settlements to gather intelligence, hire informants, and more.

### Tavern Actions

| Action | Cost | Effect |
|--------|------|--------|
| Buy Drinks | 5g | 1–3 random rumors (55% accuracy); when something is happening in the region (war, blockade, siege, plague, a saga, a rival, a new ruler, a revolt, a proclamation) most are *Talk of the Region*, and an inaccurate one is a distorted telling |
| Talk to Merchants | 10g | Prices for 3–5 goods at 2–4 nearby settlements (85% accuracy) |
| Ask about Characters | 50g | Intel on 2–3 nearest rulers (70% accuracy) |
| Ask about Military | 50g | War reports, army estimates (cities only, 70% accuracy) |
| Ask about Sacred Places | 50g | Discover 1–3 holy sites (70% accuracy) |
| Hire Informant | 200g + 10g/day | Permanent source in this city (95% accuracy) |
| Check Notice Board | Free | Kingdom decrees, settlement stats, bounties, the heralds' proclamations, talk of sagas and of [uncharted lands](#uncharted-lands) |
| Browse Maps | Free | Open cartography trade panel |
| Dice and card tables | Your stake | Opens the gambling den's games (Crown & Anchor, Dragon's Flip, High-Low, poker); a stake counts as a wager for vows and the Gambler trait |
| Sell Your Soul | Free | A named stranger buys it once for real gold; the debt comes due later — see [The Stranger (Soul Pact)](#the-stranger-soul-pact) |

### Feasts

**Host a Feast** opens a planning table (`systems/feasts.js`, `data/feasts.json`):

- **Guests** (up to 6): the rulers of realms whose seat is within 14 hexes (the town's own lord first; never one at war with you), and the town's notable people (nobles, merchants, priests, guards, smiths) from its living roster. Each lord's regard (their opinion of you plus your reputation) is shown.
- **Menu**: a modest table (8 portions of food, 4 of drink), a fine spread (16 / 8 / 2 sweets) or a grand banquet (30 / 15 / 6 spiced sweets). Each course is drawn **from your own stores first**, the rest bought at the town's market at its current price — the stalls empty, and a town that can't supply it can't host it.
- **Entertainment**: minstrels (30g), jesters (45g, insults likelier), a bard's epic of your deeds (70g, +3 renown), games of strength (40g, brawls likelier).

Each guest comes by their regard (lords) and judges the evening by the menu, the entertainment, their traits' tastes (the Greedy love a banquet, the Pious a plain table and no jesters, the Brave and the Cruel games of strength…) and their regard: **honoured** (+6 opinion), content, or **slighted** (−6). The town's own lord left off a list with other lords on it is slighted (−3). Lords remember it (the lord memory deeds `feast_honoured`, `feast_slighted`, `feast_uninvited`, `feast_insulted`, `feast_betrothal`) and your reputation in their realm moves with it; townsfolk guests' regard moves by ±12/−6.

**Incidents** (55% of feasts, sometimes two): a guest **insulted** before the hall (−8), a **betrothal** suggested by an honoured lord (promise a grown, unwed child at home — a real alliance match, which needs no standing in his realm since he offered it — or decline), a **drunken brawl** (fight it out on the duel engine: +3 renown for a win, −2 for a loss; or talk him down on charisma), a **toast** (+3 renown), or **court gossip** (you learn of the lord's realm). Renown is the menu's (3 / 6 / 12) +25% per lord who came; karma, the town's happiness and reputation with the realm rise too, and the feast is talked about (a deed rumour). **One feast a week**; a town that feasted with you in the last 30 days makes half as much of the next.

### Books

**Read** at a bookshelf takes up the book you are in the middle of, or an unread one the shelf could hold (`systems/books.js`, `data/books.json`: 14 books — a village shelf has primers, a town's more, a capital's or a temple's chronicles and lost hymnals). Each sitting advances it by one (books take 3–6 sittings; **two sittings a day count**; the Wisdom tenet reads half again as fast). Finishing a book grants its reward **once**: skills (Leadership, Commerce, Combat, Diplomacy, Stealth, Cartography), small stat gains (under a lifetime cap of +1.5 intelligence and +0.5 faith from books), an **extinct faith discovered** (the Ash Hymnal, the Songs of the Bonesingers), or the land around you charted.


| Source | Accuracy | How |
|--------|----------|-----|
| Tavern Gossip | 55% | Buying drinks |
| Local Talk | 70% | Bribing, notice board |
| Merchant Report | 85% | Merchant conversations |
| Informant | 95% | Hired informants |

Unreliable intel may report distorted prices, wrong locations, or false military estimates. Cross-reference multiple sources for confidence.

### Informants

Informants stationed in a city generate high-accuracy intel every 3 days — trade prices, political intel, and military reports. If you stop paying their 10g/day upkeep, they leave permanently.

### Rumor Management

- Maximum 40 rumors stored. Oldest are dropped when full.
- Intel decays after 30 days and is removed as stale.
- Freshness: **Fresh** (≤1 day) → **Recent** (≤7) → **Aging** (≤15) → **Stale** (>15).

### Sell Your Soul

A dark, repeatable tavern action: earn a small amount of gold (5–10g) at the cost of karma and renown. The penalties escalate with each deal — karma loss increases by 1 per 5 deals, renown loss by 1 per 3 deals. Flavor text becomes increasingly ominous over 30+ deals.

---

## 22. Quests

The game features **68 quest templates** (plus artifact quests) across 5 categories with 4 difficulty tiers (Easy, Medium, Hard, Legendary). 26 of them send you into the newer systems (see [Quests in the Newer Systems](#quests-in-the-newer-systems)).

### The Quest Board

Open the **Quest Journal** to see up to **5 offered quests** and press **Accept** to take one on (at most 5 active at once). Accepting refills the board. The board is also refreshed **every week**. A posting stays up for **30 days** (the journal shows "Posted until day N"), then comes down and isn't posted again for another 30 days. Any empty places are refilled from the catalogue. Artifact quests stay posted while you hold the fragment.

**Favours for townsfolk.** A task you accept from someone in conversation becomes a quest with a real target (see [Favours for Townsfolk](#favours-for-townsfolk)). A "reputation" task also earns **+8 reputation** with the realm you were in when you accepted it, and a "favour" task earns +3. Offers are drawn from the whole catalogue, and harder tiers unlock as you prove yourself:

| Tier | Unlocks after |
|------|---------------|
| Easy | Always |
| Medium | 2 completed quests or 25 renown |
| Hard | 5 completed quests or 100 renown |
| Legendary | 10 completed quests or 300 renown |

**Quests with a named target.** Ten board quests name a real place when you accept them, marked on the map with a gold quest plate and in the journal with its distance and direction: *Tomb of the Old Kings*, *The Deep Seam*, *The Blind Dark* and *The Drowned Halls* name the nearest unexplored tomb, mine, cave or sunken ruin of that kind (clear it to the bottom); *Ruins Explorer* names a ruin to delve; *Bandit Slayer* names the nearest outlaw lair (revealed to you; you must clear it yourself); *Dragon Hunter* sets a drake loose 6–12 hexes away to slay; *A Physician's Herbs* and *Furs for the Winter* name the town the goods must be brought to (they count only once you stand there with them); *World Traveler* names five towns you have never visited. If nothing of the kind is in reach, the quest takes its general form instead (clear any tomb, defeat 10 raider bands, visit 10 settlements…).

Artifact quests appear as soon as you find a fragment of that artifact. Progress counts everything you've done since the start of the game — battles won, ruins explored, treasure dug up, roads built, taverns visited, trade revenue and so on — so a quest may complete the moment you accept it. Heresy Hunter counts heresies you suppress with Denounce Heresy; Alliance Broker counts alliances you broker from the Wars of the Realm panel.

### Quests in the Newer Systems

These quests are offered **only when their system is within your reach** — a sea quest only if you own a ship or stand in a port, a court quest only if you hold a holding (or have petitioners waiting), a siege-relief quest only while one of your holdings is besieged or your trading company has a siege-relief contract on offer, and so on. An offer whose system slips out of reach (the ship sold, the siege over) leaves the board at the end of the day. Unlike the older quests they count only what you do **after accepting**. Delivery objectives ask you to carry goods; they are taken from your packs when the quest completes.

| Quest | Difficulty | Offered when | Objective | Reward |
|-------|-----------|--------------|-----------|--------|
| A Physician's Herbs | Easy | Always | Forage twice, bring 6 herbs | 150g, 5 renown, 2 karma |
| Furs for the Winter | Easy | Always | Hunt 3 times, bring 4 pelts | 220g, 5 renown |

### Favours for Townsfolk

Ask an NPC whether they need help and they may make a request. If you accept, it becomes a quest in your journal (it counts toward the 5-quest limit) with a **real target**, marked on the world map with a gold quest plate and in the journal (📍 with its distance and direction):

| Request | Target |
|---------|--------|
| A merchant's missing caravan | the outlaw lair nearby that took it, else a band of brigands near town — recover the strongbox |
| A merchant's spice supplier | a merchant in another town — carry the letter of terms, bring back the reply |
| A guard's bandit camp | the nearest outlaw lair (revealed to you), else an outlaw band near town |
| A guard's lost report | someone in the same town who picked it up |
| A farmer's dead livestock | a beast den nearby, else a wolf pack near town |
| A farmer's missing son | the farmer's **real son** (from the town's roll; he is gone from town while missing) — in an unexplored ruin or cave nearby (or one nobody had found, close by), else held by outlaws |
| A priest's stolen relic | a ruin, tomb or sunken hall nearby (or a forgotten one), else a band of thieves |
| A priest's restless dead | a tomb nearby, else the dead walking near town |
| A blacksmith's rare ore | a mine or cave nearby (star-iron at the bottom), else 6 iron |
| A blacksmith's stolen sword | a band of cutpurses near town |
| A villager's grandmother's locket | the bandit camp nearby, else a band of cutpurses |
| A villager's medicine for the hermit | someone in a small town nearby |
| A noble's rival's documents | a noble in another town — who won't simply hand them over: buy them (80 gold) or lift them (Stealth against 5; caught, they will hate you) |
| A noble's challenge | a noble in another town — deliver it, bring back the answer |
| A traveller's ruins | a ruin, tomb or sunken hall nearby |
| A traveller's bounty | a named fugitive near town |

- **Bands** spawned for a task are real hostile units on the map; they must be beaten by **you**. If a patrol or anyone else scatters them they regroup near their old haunt (up to three times).
- **A lair** must be cleared by you; if the realm's wardens burn it first, or it scatters, the task fails. **A delve site** must be entered at least one chamber deep (turn back at the mouth and whatever you sought is lost).
- **People in other towns** are real townsfolk of that town when you have walked its streets (its roll), else a named stranger the town knows. If the person isn't about, any townsperson there will take a delivery or hand over what was left for you. Talk to them and choose the task's line (in the dialog's **Your Task** group).
- **Bring it back.** When you find the thing (or the son), you carry it (🎒 in the journal) and must **return to the person who asked** — find them in their town and choose *"I've brought back …"*. They pay the reward there and then, like you more (+15 disposition), and remember it (a son brought home is back in town and remembers it too). A traveller who asked has no home to go back to, so their task completes as soon as the target is done.
- **The other way out.** Some tasks can end differently, from the journal once you have what was sought: **sell** the locket to a fence (60 gold, −3 karma) or the relic to a collector (180 gold, −6 karma), **keep** the strongbox (160 gold) or the knight's sword, hand the lost report to the captain (40 gold), sell the rival's documents back to their owner (100 gold), or **let the son go** his own way (he moves to another town; you keep the renown, +1 karma). The person who asked hears of it: they like you far less (−30), won't offer you another task, and tell you so when next you meet.
- **Deadline.** A task left for 90 days fails (the journal counts the days down), as does one that can no longer be done; the person who asked is let down (−12 disposition, and they say so). Failed tasks are listed in the journal.
- **Older saves:** a general task from before ("explore any ruin") is given a real target from its giver's home on the first day after loading, where one can be found; otherwise it keeps working as before.

The reward depends on what they promised: gold, renown, karma, or forged weapons from a blacksmith.

## 23. Achievements

They are checked at the end of every day; each one reads something the game really records (a counter in Deeds & Records, a system's live state, or an event counter), so every one can be earned. **Journal → Deeds & Records** lists the achievements you have won by tier, each with the day it was won, and your overall progress.

## 24. World Events

The world is alive with dynamic events that affect all kingdoms:

### Timed Events

Some events last for days or weeks — droughts, plagues, floods, gold rushes, trade booms, market crashes, pirate surges, bandit uprisings, blizzards, hungry months and more (24 in all). Natural events follow the seasons: floods in spring, droughts in summer, bountiful harvests in autumn, blizzards and famine in the lean months. Each day there's a chance one begins, either in a kingdom (two kingdoms for wars and royal weddings) or across the whole realm; at most four run at once. The tile info panel lists the events affecting a tile, what they do, and how long they have left.

What the effects do:
- **Farm/mine output, prices, trade income, caravan traffic, raiders, pirates, ship upkeep** — your farms and mines, market prices, caravan and port profits, how many caravans/bandits/pirates appear (bandits gather near a troubled settlement), and your ships' daily upkeep.
- **Population** — a one-off loss when the event strikes. **Population growth / food consumption** — the region's settlements slowly grow or shrink while it lasts.
- **Buildings damaged** — the struck settlement produces less gold and goods until builders repair it (about 1% a day; faster with construction bonuses).
- **Tax income** — both the kingdom's income and the taxes you collect there.
- **Stability / morale** — move the kingdom's stability target; morale also shifts battle morale for kingdoms and for you (±0.2 at ±20).
- **Travel speed** — your daily movement while you're in the region. **Army attrition** — your army loses men each day it camps outside a settlement; kingdom armies shrink.
- **Diplomacy / relations, piety, heresy risk, war spending, renown from victories** — kingdom diplomacy drift, daily relation gains between wedded houses, the kingdom's piety, the daily chance of a new heresy, war upkeep, and extra renown for your victories.

### Natural Events
| Event | Severity | Effect |
|-------|----------|--------|
| Drought | Moderate | −40% farm output, −20% growth, +50% food prices, −5 stability |
| Plague | Severe | −15% population, −50% trade, −50% growth, −20 morale, −10 stability |
| Flood | Moderate | −30% farm output, −30% travel speed, 10% building damage |
| Earthquake | Severe | 25% building damage, −5% population, −15 morale, −5 stability |
| Volcanic Eruption | Catastrophic | −20% population, −60% farming, 30% building damage, −20% travel |
| Bountiful Harvest | Positive | +50% farm output, −30% food prices, +30% growth, +10 morale |
| Blizzard | Moderate | −50% travel speed, −20% farming, 10% army attrition |
| Hungry Months | Moderate | +40% food prices, −30% growth, −15% farming, −6 stability, −8 morale (lean months only) |
| Comet Sighting | Neutral | −5 morale, +1 piety/day, −3 stability (realm-wide) |
| Solar Eclipse | Neutral | −10 morale, +2 piety/day, −8 stability (realm-wide) |

### Economic Events
| Event | Severity | Effect |
|-------|----------|--------|
| Gold Rush | Positive | +60% mine output, +30% growth, +20% bandits, +15% taxes |
| Trade Boom | Positive | +40% trade income, +30% caravans, −15% prices |
| Market Crash | Moderate | −40% trade income, −20% taxes, −10 morale |

### Military Events

|-------|----------|--------|

| Bandit Uprising | Moderate | +50% bandits, −30% land trade, −15 stability |
| Kingdom War | Severe | Two hostile kingdoms go to war (truce when it ends); +50% war spending, −10% population, −30% trade |
| Monster Sighting | Moderate | −15% travel speed, −10 morale, +50% renown from victories |

Smaller military news is reported too. **Training exercises** add up to 10% to a realm's army, but never more than the realm can feed (its cap). **Desertions** cost 10% of the army. **Recruitment drives** add 1% of the population, up to the cap. **Parades** raise stability a little.

## Court Scandals & Whispers

The great are talked about. From about day 20, a new tale hatches every 14–28 days, with at most 3 going at once. Most tales are about a **ruler** and some are about a **rival adventurer**. Some are true and some are lies. Nobody knows which, including you, until you find out.

| Tale | About | Likelier true when… |
|---|---|---|
| 👶 A Hidden Bastard | rulers | — |
| 🧪 A Poisoning | rulers | the ruler is cruel or cunning |
| 💸 The Crown's Debts | rulers | the treasury is nearly empty, or the ruler is greedy |
| 🕯️ A Secret Heresy | rulers | the ruler is cunning (less if pious) |
| 📜 A Secret Pact with the enemy | rulers at war | the ruler is cunning or cautious |
| 🐔 Cowardice | rulers, rivals | the ruler is cautious (less if brave); a rival who has lost more duels than they've won |
| 💋 An Affair | rulers, rivals | — |
| 🌀 Madness | rulers | the ruler is 55 or older |
| 🎲 A Cheat · ⚰️ Grave-Robbing | rivals | a rival with 3+ delves (grave-robbing) |

**Spread and heat.** A tale starts in one town and spreads to nearby towns (up to 7 hexes away). Its **heat** (0–100) is 12 per town of the subject's own realm that tells it, about 5 per foreign town, plus whatever was paid to fan or quash it. Past the halfway point of its 35–60 day life it cools by 0.8 a day. A hot tale about a ruler drags on their realm's stability (up to −12 at full heat, scaled by the kind of tale; −20 at most per realm), shown as *Court scandal* in the realm's stability breakdown.

**How tales end.** A **true** tale still at heat 50+ when its time is up is **proven**: −8 stability for a ruler's realm, −12 renown for a rival. A **lie** that ran hot (30+) or was quashed is **debunked**: the crown is vindicated (+3 stability). Anything else is **forgotten**.

**Each tale has its own fallout.** On top of the stability shock, a ruler's tale proven (or debunked) does what its kind would do: a **heresy** proven costs the realm's faith 15 unity and 6 piety (debunked: +4 piety); a **secret pact** proven sours the realm and the named enemy on each other (−20 relations both ways; a forgery exposed, −5); **the crown's debts** proven send the lenders for their money (−15% of the treasury; debunked, +1 stability); **madness**, a **poisoning** or a **hidden bastard** proven unsettle the royal council over the succession (every advisor's loyalty −12 / −10 / −8, and an advisor under 30 drags on the realm); **cowardice** and an **affair** cost the council's loyalty a little. The notice names the fallout.

**A lie made true.** A lie you started or fanned that runs at heat 50+ for **8 days on end** comes to a reckoning. It is either **believed for good** (proven, with all a proven tale's effects, though it was never so) or **debunked** there and then. The chance it is believed is 30%, +4% per town of the subject's own realm that tells it, −0.5% per point of the ruler's stability above 50 (+ below it), or the same by a rival's renown, between 5% and 80%. The tale's card shows how many days it has run hot. Debunking is the riskier end for you (below).

**Hearing them.** You hear a tale on arriving in a town that tells it. The townsfolk repeat it, and guards, priests and merchants each take it their own way. In a tavern, **🕯️ The Whispering Corner** lists the tales told there, the talk about you as it is told in that town, and how past tales ended. There you can:

| Act | Where | Cost | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🔥 **Fan it** | A town that tells it | 40g | +15 heat and 3 more chances to spread. The crowns at war with the subject's realm like you for it (+3 each). There is a 5% chance the subject's men notice (−8 with that realm) |
| 🤫 **Quash it** | The subject's realm | 60g | −30 heat. +4 reputation with a ruler; a rival remembers you spoke up for them |
| 🔍 **Dig for the truth** | A town that tells it | 30g | 35% + 5% per point of your best of Intelligence and Charisma; once a day per tale. A **close friend** in the realm may simply tell you (a *Confidence*) |
| ✉️ **Blackmail** | The subject's realm, a ruler's tale you know is true | — | 120g + 3 per heat point, −15 with that realm, and the tale is buried. An **honourable** or **brave** ruler may instead (60%) hand your letter to the guard: a *Blackmail* crime rumour and a 150g bounty, and the tale runs hotter |
| 📜 **Sell the proof** | Any tavern, a ruler's tale you know is true | — | To a crown at war with the subject's realm: 80g + 2 per heat point, +8 with the buyer, −10 with the subject's realm, and the tale is **proven** on the spot |
| 🕯️ **Start a tale** | Any tavern | 80g | A tale of your choosing about any living ruler or a rival you've met (one tale per subject at a time), starting at 12 heat. It may happen to be true without your knowing it |

**Getting caught.** A tale you started can be traced back to you: each day there is a 2.5% chance, scaled up by its heat and down to 40% of that if the tale is true. If a lie is debunked, there is a further 50% chance it is traced, +20% if you also fanned it, and a 20% chance for a lie you only fanned (you are named as the one who paid to keep it going). Fanning a tale you started makes the daily chance half as large again. Once traced, a *Whisperer Exposed* crime rumour starts in the town where you began it, you lose 15 reputation with the subject's realm (5 if the tale was true), a rival's attitude toward you drops by 35, and a lie is debunked at once. The corner's footer, and Deeds & Records, keep your tally: tales planted, fanned and quashed, lies believed and debunked, whispers traced, rulers blackmailed and proofs sold.

## Lords' Memory & Court Letters

**The lords remember.** Every ruler keeps a memory of what you have done to them and their realm (`world/npcLords.js`, the deed table in `data/lordMemory.json`). Each deed carries a weight and a half-life: a purse of gold is worth +4 and is half-forgotten in 120 days; a broken oath is −25 and lasts two years; a town torn from the realm is −30 for most of three. The memories add up (capped at ±40) into the lord's **opinion** of you — the same opinion the royal audience's mood ring, your odds of being granted trade terms or soldiers, and a claim's support at court read — and into how much a realm **wants war with you** (a lord who remembers wrongs is quicker to send an ultimatum). The same deed repeated within five days adds to the last one, up to three times its weight.

What lords remember: gifts and treasures laid before them, oaths sworn and renounced (going over to another crown counts as half a broken oath with the old one), threats and extorted tribute, raids on their towns (and failed raids), failed assaults and conquests, patrols cut down, wars between you, peace bought or forced on them, tribute paid, your sword in their wars (each campaign win for your liege — and the enemy remembers it too), truces and alliances you brokered for them, a crown restored to a fallen realm (+30) or rebels paid against it, marriage into their nobility, a child wed or fostered at their court, and every answer to their letters below.

**"My father spoke of you."** When a lord dies, the heir inherits half of what the old lord remembered (anything that would be worth less than 2 is forgotten). In audience the heir says so.

In the **royal audience** the lord's card lists the three memories that weigh most ("He remembers the oath you renounced", "Her mother, Queen Maud, spoke of the crown you restored to the realm"), and a lord with a strong memory opens with it. **Journal → Letters → What Lords Remember** lists every ruler's memories of you and what they add up to.

**Court letters.** The great write to you (`systems/courtLetters.js`, `data/courtLetters.json`). At most one new letter comes every 3–7 days, and no more than four wait open at once; each has a deadline, and **silence has a price**. Letters arrive with a notice and wait in **Journal → Letters** (also from Dynasty & Heirs); the Journal entry shows a count of unread letters.

| Letter | From | When | Answers |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🏅 An office at court | your liege | you are sworn, they think well of you (opinion + reputation ≥ 5), and the next office up is within reach with its reputation/renown waived | accept (appointed, +5 memory) · decline (−4) |
| 💍 A match for your child | a realm at peace with you (reputation ≥ 0) | a grown, unwed child at home | accept: the child weds into that realm, **they pay you a dowry** of 100–300 gold (+10 memory) · refuse (−8) |
| 🍖 A feast | any lord at peace with you who thinks well enough of you | — | attend with a gift (30–70 gold): +2 renown, +4 reputation, you learn of the ruler and their diplomacy, +5 memory · send regrets (−2) |
| 🏇 A tournament | any lord at peace | — | ride (entry 20–50 gold): three bouts on the duel engine, fought on instinct, for a purse of 150–400 (+3 memory, +7 as champion) · watch · decline |
| ⚔️ A call to arms | your liege at war | — | **promise to ride**: win a campaign battle against that enemy for your liege (Realm Politics → Campaign) within 30 days — kept: +6 memory; broken: −12 memory and −10 reputation · send gold (10% of your purse, 100–1500) to the war chest (it buys soldiers, and 300+ pushes the war goal) · refuse (−15 memory, −10 reputation) |
| 🏦 A loan | a lord at peace with you | you could lend 15% of your purse (100–2000) and keep half | lend: repaid on the due day (60–120 days) with 8–20% interest — likelier from an honourable lord with a full treasury, less from an heir; a default brings a letter begging patience: forgive (+10 memory) or demand (maybe half back, the lord resents it) |
| 🕊️ A truce | a rival House with feud ≥ 30 | — | accept (feud −25) · refuse (+5) |
| 💀 A tribute demand | a rival House with feud ≥ 50 | — | pay 5% of your purse (feud −20) · refuse (+10) · send back an insult (+3 renown, feud +20) |
| 💍 A match to end the feud | a rival House with feud ≥ 20 | a grown, unwed child | accept: the child weds into the rival's realm, feud −60 · refuse (+8) |
| 💌 A plea | your spouse | your realm is at war with the spouse's homeland | speak for peace (affection +12; your liege's regard −5; the two crowns' relations warm) · offer terms (your own war: buy the peace, affection +24) · duty first (affection −15) |
| 📨 News from abroad | a child married or fostered abroad | at peace with that realm | send a gift (+3 reputation there) · ask what is said at court (you learn its ruler, army, diplomacy and economy) |
| ⛓️ A child afraid | the same child | war with that realm | bring them home (ransom; that crown takes offence, −8 memory) · trust their spouse's kin (+4 memory — but in 20 days, if that crown thinks ill of you, the child is **held as a hostage**: −8 Prestige, −10 reputation) |

**Blood feuds.** Some wrongs are not half-forgotten: a town torn from the realm, a raid, a broken oath or promise, extorted tribute, a peace forced on them, rebels paid against them, letters tampered with, a scandal about the throne traced back to you, a ruler's kin assassinated (or your assassin caught at court). These are **vendetta** deeds (marked in `data/lordMemory.json`): when the lord dies his heir inherits them at 85% instead of half, and the feud counts its generations ("sworn a generation ago"). A realm whose lord owes you blood (15 or more) is sworn as a **rival House** with its feud kept at least that hot — and it plots, buys agents in your towns and joins leagues against you (see [Coalitions & Threats](#coalitions--threats)).

Letters left unanswered: most cost −3 memory with the sender; an ignored call to arms −20 memory and −15 reputation; an ignored rival letter heats the feud; an ignored spouse's plea costs 20 affection; an ignored child's fear counts as trusting their in-laws, without the credit. **Journal → Letters** also lists the letters you answered, your loans to crowns and the promises you have given. Loans move through the Merchant's Ledger as *Loans to lords* and *Interest from lords' loans*. The inbox, loans and promises are saved with your game; a lord's memories are saved with the kingdom.

## Coalitions & Threats

The realms watch you grow. While you are a wanderer nobody minds you; once you are a power, the courts start counting — and when you tower over them, the ones who fear and envy you most swear a **league** against you. Open **Dynasty → ⚜️ Threats** (also at the top of **Realm Wars**) to see where every enemy stands. (`systems/coalitions.js`, `systems/rivalAgents.js`, tuning in `data/coalitions.json`.)

**Your power, as the courts measure it** (reckoned weekly):

| Source | Points |
|---|---|
| Towns held in your own name and colonies | 12 each |
| Properties | 1.5 each (up to 30) |
| A crown you rule | 40, +4 per town of the realm |
| A royal office | 8 |
| Your land army | 1 per 25 strength (up to 60) |
| Net worth | 1 per 1,500 gold (up to 50) |
| House Prestige | 1 per 8 (up to 40) |
| Renown | 1 per 10 (up to 25) |
| Towns you conquered in the last year | 12 each (up to 48) |

The realms start to watch at **70**; past **140** for two weekly reckonings in a row, a league may be sworn. Both marks are divided by your difficulty's *hostility* knob (Easy raises them, Hard and Brutal lower them), and `difficultyScale` in the data file scales them for everyone. A starting wanderer stands at about 0–10.
