| Restore the Family House | earn 600 trade profit | 📦 Quartermaster: +4% trade |
| Taken at Sea | defeat pirates at sea | ⚓ Sea Legs: +4% battle power, +2% trade |
| The Stolen Manuscript | hire an informant, explore a ruin | 📜 Lorekeeper: +1.5 diplomacy |
| A Name Cleared | drive off raiders twice | 🗣️ Silver Tongue: +1 diplomacy |
| The Healer's Oath | give 150 gold of charity through them | 🩹 Field Surgeon: +3 heal |

Fulfil the quest and the companion becomes **Devoted**. Their approval goes to 100 and they stay Devoted whatever you do after that. They never complain or leave, and their perk lasts. The House chronicle remembers it. If a companion leaves or dies, their quest is dropped.

**Bonds.** Companions who travel together for 20 days become **friends** or **rivals**. Shared values make friends and clashing values make rivals, such as greed against compassion or freedom against loyalty. Each friend in the party makes a companion 5% more effective, and each rival makes them 5% less.

**The fallen.** Companions fight beside you. In every battle each one has a small chance to fall: 1% in a victory and 3% in a defeat, half again more for fighters. The party mourns them, their friends lose approval, and the House chronicle records it. The roster keeps a list of the fallen.

**The roster.** *Travel Party* (top bar or the armies rail) shows each companion's mood, approval, values, bonds, wage, current gripe and quest progress. From there you can **Talk** (once a day, +2 approval, and they tell you what they care about), give a **Gift** (40 gold, +6 approval or +9 for the greedy, once every 3 days), **Pay** wages early, **Grant** a raise, hear out or pay toward a personal quest, or **Dismiss** them.

**Rumours of your crimes.** When gossip about a crime reaches a settlement, its guards may turn hostile and its crown may post a bounty on you. If the rumour is bad enough, the settlement **bans** you. The worst crimes bring a **warrant** that covers the whole kingdom. While you're banned or wanted, merchants, taverns, recruiters and officials turn you away. Only the guard tower will still deal with you, so you can settle your record there. Bounties and warrants lapse after 90 days if you don't pay them.

**Settlement food.** Most townsfolk grow their own food, so only the gap matters: villages run a small surplus and grow slowly towards a size cap (village 1,500 · town 5,000 · city 15,000 · capital 30,000); towns and cities buy their shortfall with their own gold. A settlement that is both short of food and broke starves and shrinks, gradually.

**Diplomacy Mastery** also makes gifts to lords buy more goodwill and softens a former crown's anger when you pledge to another. **Explorer** rulers scout further afield and survey more sites when founding colonies.


## Craft, Brawls, Contracts and Small Debts

**Crafted quality shows and counts.** Goods from your own bench carry their quality tier (🧱 Crude, 🔷 Fine, 🌟 Masterwork; Common is not marked) in the inventory, the HUD pack, the hotbar tooltip and the sell rows of a market or a trader, whose prices include it. In a duel (training, tournaments, rival duels, a rival suitor) a crafted sword's damage and attack, a shield's guard and armour's protection are scaled by their quality: a Masterwork blade cuts half again as deep, a Crude one three-quarters.

**Townsfolk brawl by who they are.** Attack someone on a town map and whether they stand or run comes from their duel profile (attack + defence), their temperament (the bold and stubborn stand, the cautious and quiet run), how dangerous you look and how hurt they already are: a guard stands, a merchant runs, a bold traveller may stand. A fighter's counter-blows are their profile's swing (a guard's spear hits harder than a court sword), softened by your armour.

**The street sees what you do.** Beat or kill someone on a town map and everyone within about seven tiles on the street (or everyone in the room, indoors) sees it. They cry out over their heads, then run for the edge of town, freeze where they stand, or stand and shout; the victim's family may come at you; guards who saw it, or heard the cries from further off, come running. A guard who reaches you stops you: give yourself up (the cells; the matter is closed with the watch and the realm's bounties dropped), pay the fine (a beating only, never less than the bounty you owe there), run for it (stealth helps; caught, and you serve longer), or resist. An assault and a murder are distinct crimes: each is a rumour told by its real witnesses (a beaten victim bears witness too), heat with the crown, cruelty and dread on your name, and karma. A killing no one saw makes no rumour, but the body is found: some heat, and the karma all the same. Witnesses remember. Speak to one afterwards and they name what you did and to whom, in fear or in anger; one who saw a killing will not talk or trade with you until you make amends with gifts. Family who were not on the street that day hear of it from the town and hold the grudge on the roll, so the widow remembers you years later. Those who saw nothing hear of it as the rumour spreads, more of the town each day, and mention it more vaguely; so do towns the rumour reaches. The watch in that town remembers an unanswered crime for a month: come back within it and the nearest guard comes for you. (Tuned and worded in `data/streetCrime.json`.)

**A rival suitor is a real fight.** "I'll have a word with them, outside" is fought on the duel engine against the suitor's own kind (a captain of the guard fights like a guard, a travelling poet like a traveller); the odds shown are that duel's, and a beating hurts by how badly it went.

**A deed is worth its telling.** Each town a deed of yours reaches raises your reputation with that town's realm once (up to +5 for a fresh legend, less as it ages; a sack wins no one's love). A friend there warms by the same measure. A deed told as **legend** counts for more, a **soured** telling for little, and one **credited to another** for nothing.

**The roll of deeds.** Deeds & Records now counts the wilds (encounters met and dealt with, risks that paid off or went wrong, places explored, with the last entries of the wilds journal), your crafting (things made, masterworks, spoiled attempts, your crafter's rank) and jobs completed. A Masterwork from your own hands wins the **Masterwork** achievement.

**Mercenary contracts.** Each contract has a loot table: a strongbox on the march may hold goods as well as gold, and a won contract may bring spoils (a pirate cove's spices, gems and liquor; a ruin's relics). What doesn't fit in the packs is sold on the spot. Raiding a pirate cove counts 80% of your war galleys' strength alongside the army. The jobs panel shows what has happened on the march, and the completion notice tells it.

**Tasks and favours.** Handing the rival's documents back to their owner instead earns their thanks (they think better of you, thank you when next you speak, and your name rises a little in their realm) and +1 karma; your employer still hears of it. A townsperson's thanks for a task warm them by the amount in the data. The coin a friend presses on you as a favour is a loan: pay it back from the conversation within 30 days and they think the better of you; let it run past due and they ask for it, a little colder. A guard or traveller who likes you well knows the land further out (outlaw lairs up to 16 hexes) and names two old places instead of one.

**Withdrawing from a field battle** leaves the band that fought you alone for 3 days.
